   312	function validateRuntime(value, label, errors) {
   313	  if (!isObject(value)) { errors.push(`${label} must be an object`); return; }
   314	  if (!hasOnlyKeys(value, RUNTIME_KEYS)) errors.push(`${label} contains unknown fields`);
   315	  for (const key of RUNTIME_KEYS) if (!nonempty(value[key])) errors.push(`${label}.${key} must be non-empty`);
   316	}
   317	
   318	function validateInspector(value, label, errors) {
   319	  if (!isObject(value)) { errors.push(`${label} is required for manual evidence`); return; }
   320	  if (!hasOnlyKeys(value, INSPECTOR_KEYS)) errors.push(`${label} contains unknown fields`);
   321	  for (const key of INSPECTOR_KEYS) if (!nonempty(value[key])) errors.push(`${label}.${key} must be non-empty`);
   322	}
   323	
   324	function validateReviewer(value, label, errors) {
   325	  if (!isObject(value)) { errors.push(`${label} is required`); return; }
   326	  if (!hasOnlyKeys(value, REVIEWER_KEYS)) errors.push(`${label} contains unknown fields`);
   327	  if (!nonempty(value.id)) errors.push(`${label}.id must be non-empty`);
   328	  if (value.type !== "human") errors.push(`${label}.type must be human`);
   329	  if (!nonempty(value.role)) errors.push(`${label}.role must be non-empty`);
   330	  if (!nonempty(value.method)) errors.push(`${label}.method must be non-empty`);
   331	}
   332	
   333	function validateAdjudication(value, status, reviewer, errors) {
   334	  if (!isObject(value)) { errors.push("subjective disagreement requires adjudication"); return; }
   335	  if (!hasOnlyKeys(value, ADJUDICATION_KEYS)) errors.push("adjudication contains unknown fields");
   336	  validateReviewer(value.adjudicator, "adjudication.adjudicator", errors);
   337	  if (value.adjudicator?.id === reviewer?.id) errors.push("adjudicator must be independent from the primary reviewer");
   338	  if (!nonempty(value.rule)) errors.push("adjudication.rule must be non-empty");
   339	  if (value.decision !== status) errors.push("adjudication.decision must match the row status");
   340	  if (!ISO_DATE.test(value.resolved_at ?? "") || Number.isNaN(Date.parse(value.resolved_at))) errors.push("adjudication.resolved_at must be an ISO-8601 UTC timestamp");
   341	  if (!nonempty(value.rationale)) errors.push("adjudication.rationale must be non-empty");
   342	}
   343	
   344	function validateReview(review, check, status, provenance, errors) {
   345	  if (check.judgment !== "subjective") {
   346	    if (review !== undefined && review !== null) errors.push(`${check.id}: deterministic rows may not carry reviewer judgment`);
   347	    return;
   348	  }
   349	  if (!isObject(review)) { errors.push(`${check.id}: subjective rows require reviewer provenance`); return; }
   350	  if (!hasOnlyKeys(review, REVIEW_KEYS)) errors.push(`${check.id}: review contains unknown fields`);
   351	  validateReviewer(review.reviewer, `${check.id}: review.reviewer`, errors);
   352	  if (!isObject(review.rubric)) errors.push(`${check.id}: review.rubric is required`);
   353	  else {
   354	    if (!hasOnlyKeys(review.rubric, ["id", "version", "criteria"])) errors.push(`${check.id}: review.rubric contains unknown fields`);
   355	    if (!nonempty(review.rubric.id)) errors.push(`${check.id}: review.rubric.id must be non-empty`);
   356	    if (!nonempty(review.rubric.version)) errors.push(`${check.id}: review.rubric.version must be non-empty`);
   357	    if (!("criteria" in review.rubric)) errors.push(`${check.id}: review.rubric.criteria is required`);
   358	  }
   359	  if (review.independent !== true) errors.push(`${check.id}: subjective review must be marked independent=true`);
   360	  if (review.decision !== status) errors.push(`${check.id}: review.decision must match the row status`);
   361	  if (typeof review.disagreement !== "boolean") errors.push(`${check.id}: review.disagreement must be boolean`);
   362	  if (!ISO_DATE.test(review.reviewed_at ?? "") || Number.isNaN(Date.parse(review.reviewed_at))) errors.push(`${check.id}: review.reviewed_at must be an ISO-8601 UTC timestamp`);
   363	  if (review.disagreement === true) validateAdjudication(review.adjudication, status, review.reviewer, errors);
   364	  else if (review.adjudication !== null) errors.push(`${check.id}: adjudication must be null when reviewers do not disagree`);
   365	  if (review.reviewer?.id && provenance?.tool?.name && review.reviewer.id.toLowerCase() === provenance.tool.name.toLowerCase()) errors.push(`${check.id}: reviewer must not self-certify as the execution tool`);
   366	  if (!SHA256.test(review.sha256 ?? "")) errors.push(`${check.id}: review.sha256 must be a lowercase SHA-256`);
   367	  else if (hashReview(review) !== review.sha256) errors.push(`${check.id}: review.sha256 does not match canonical review content`);
   368	}
   369	
   370	function validateEvidence(evidence, check, status, errors) {
   371	  if (!isObject(evidence)) { errors.push("evidence must be an object"); return; }
   372	  if (!hasOnlyKeys(evidence, ["mode", "summary", "details", "subject", "subject_absent", "sha256"])) errors.push("evidence contains unknown fields");
   373	  if (!["automated", "manual"].includes(evidence.mode)) errors.push("evidence.mode must be automated or manual");
   374	  if (!nonempty(evidence.summary)) errors.push("evidence.summary must be non-empty");
   375	  if (!("details" in evidence) || evidence.details === undefined || evidence.details === null) errors.push("evidence.details is required");
   376	  if (!SHA256.test(evidence.sha256 ?? "")) errors.push("evidence.sha256 must be a lowercase SHA-256");
   377	  else if (hashEvidence(evidence) !== evidence.sha256) errors.push("evidence.sha256 does not match canonical evidence content");
   378	  if (check.manual_inspector_required && evidence.mode !== "manual") errors.push(`${check.id}: manual evidence is required`);
   379	  if (status === "n/a") {
   380	    if (check.na_policy !== "subject_absence") errors.push(`${check.id}: required row may not use n/a`);
   381	    if (evidence.subject_absent !== true) errors.push(`${check.id}: n/a requires subject_absent=true evidence`);
   382	    if (evidence.subject !== check.applicability.subject) errors.push(`${check.id}: n/a subject must be ${check.applicability.subject}`);
   383	  }
   384	}
   385	
   386	function validateProvenance(provenance, artifact, evidence, check, errors) {
   387	  if (!isObject(provenance)) { errors.push("provenance must be an object"); return; }
   388	  if (!hasOnlyKeys(provenance, ["artifact_identity", "artifact_sha256", "captured_at", "tool", "browser", "inspector", "sha256"])) errors.push("provenance contains unknown fields");
   389	  if (provenance.artifact_identity !== artifact.identity) errors.push(`${check.id}: provenance artifact identity does not match measured artifact`);
   390	  if (provenance.artifact_sha256 !== artifact.sha256) errors.push(`${check.id}: provenance artifact SHA-256 does not match measured artifact`);
   391	  if (!ISO_DATE.test(provenance.captured_at ?? "") || Number.isNaN(Date.parse(provenance.captured_at))) errors.push(`${check.id}: captured_at must be an ISO-8601 UTC timestamp`);
   392	  validateRuntime(provenance.tool, `${check.id}: provenance.tool`, errors);
   393	  validateRuntime(provenance.browser, `${check.id}: provenance.browser`, errors);
   394	  if (evidence?.mode === "manual") validateInspector(provenance.inspector, `${check.id}: provenance.inspector`, errors);
   395	  else if (provenance.inspector !== null) errors.push(`${check.id}: automated evidence must set provenance.inspector to null`);
   396	  if (!SHA256.test(provenance.sha256 ?? "")) errors.push(`${check.id}: provenance.sha256 must be a lowercase SHA-256`);
   397	  else if (hashProvenance(provenance) !== provenance.sha256) errors.push(`${check.id}: provenance.sha256 does not match canonical provenance content`);
   398	}
   399	
   400	function missingRow(check) {
   401	  return {
   402	    skill: SKILL,
   403	    check_id: check.id,
   404	    status: "fail",
   405	    reason: "No ledger row was supplied for this closed check.",
   406	    remediation: "Run this check on the real artifact and attach complete evidence and provenance.",
   407	    evidence: null,
   408	    provenance: null,
   409	    review: null,
   410	    validation_errors: ["missing ledger row"],
   411	  };
   412	}
   413	
   414	function normalizeRow(row, check, artifact) {
   415	  const errors = [];
   416	  if (!isObject(row)) return missingRow(check);
   417	  if (!hasOnlyKeys(row, ["skill", "check_id", "status", "reason", "remediation", "evidence", "provenance", "review"])) errors.push(`${check.id}: ledger row contains unknown fields`);
   418	  const status = ["pass", "fail", "n/a"].includes(row.status) ? row.status : "fail";
   419	  const safeEvidence = row.evidence === null || row.evidence === undefined ? row.evidence : redactUntrusted(row.evidence);
   420	  const safeProvenance = row.provenance === null || row.provenance === undefined ? row.provenance : redactUntrusted(row.provenance);
   421	  const safeReview = row.review === null || row.review === undefined ? row.review : redactUntrusted(row.review);
   422	  if (row.skill !== SKILL) errors.push(`${check.id}: skill must be ${SKILL}`);
   423	  if (!["pass", "fail", "n/a"].includes(row.status)) errors.push("status must be pass, fail, or n/a");
   424	  for (const field of ["reason", "remediation"]) if (!nonempty(row[field])) errors.push(`${field} must be non-empty`);
   425	  validateEvidence(safeEvidence, check, status, errors);
   426	  validateProvenance(safeProvenance, artifact, safeEvidence, check, errors);
   427	  validateReview(safeReview, check, status, safeProvenance, errors);
   428	  return { skill: SKILL, check_id: check.id, status, reason: redactCapturedText(row.reason || "Invalid ledger row."), remediation: redactCapturedText(row.remediation || "Repair the ledger row and rerun the release gate."), evidence: safeEvidence ?? null, provenance: safeProvenance ?? null, review: safeReview ?? null, validation_errors: errors };
   429	}
   430	
   431	function structuralInputErrors(input) {
   432	  const errors = [];
   433	  if (!isObject(input)) return ["ledger input must be an object"];
   434	  if (!hasOnlyKeys(input, ["schema_version", "catalog", "artifact", "execution", "rows"])) errors.push("ledger input contains unknown fields");
   435	  if (input.schema_version !== SCHEMA_VERSION) errors.push("ledger schema_version must be 1");
   436	  if (!isObject(input.artifact)) errors.push("ledger artifact is required");
   437	  if (!Array.isArray(input.rows)) errors.push("ledger rows must be an array");
   438	  if (input.catalog !== undefined && (!isObject(input.catalog) || !hasOnlyKeys(input.catalog, ["path", "sha256"]))) errors.push("ledger catalog must contain only path and sha256");
   439	  return errors;
   440	}
   441	
   442	export function evaluateReleaseGate(input, { root = ROOT } = {}) {
   443	  const errors = structuralInputErrors(input);
   444	  const execution = assessExecutionPolicy(input?.execution);
   445	  errors.push(...execution.errors.map((error) => `execution: ${error}`));
   446	  let loaded;
   447	  try { loaded = loadCheckCatalog({ root }); }
   448	  catch (error) {
   449	    return {
   450	      schema_version: SCHEMA_VERSION, kind: KIND,
   451	      catalog: { path: CATALOG_PATH, sha256: "0".repeat(64), check_ids: [] },
   452	      artifact: { type: "file", identity: "unresolved", sha256: "0".repeat(64), bytes: 0, hash_verified: false },
   453	      execution: execution.policy,
   454	      rows: [], verdict: "HOLD", release_eligible: false, blockers: ["catalog", ...(execution.allowed ? [] : ["execution"])],
   455	      validation: { input_valid: false, execution_policy_valid: execution.allowed, catalog_complete: false, artifact_hash_verified: false, evidence_hashes_verified: false, provenance_hashes_verified: false, errors: [`cannot load check catalog: ${error.message}`, ...execution.errors.map((item) => `execution: ${item}`)] },
   456	    };
   457	  }
   458	  errors.push(...loaded.errors.map((error) => `catalog: ${error}`));
   459	  if (isObject(input?.catalog)) {
   460	    if (input.catalog.path !== CATALOG_PATH) errors.push(`catalog path must be ${CATALOG_PATH}`);
   461	    if (input.catalog.sha256 !== loaded.sha256) errors.push("input catalog SHA-256 does not match the canonical catalog");
   462	  }
   463	  const inspected = inspectArtifact(input?.artifact, { root });
   464	  errors.push(...inspected.errors.map((error) => `artifact: ${error}`));
   465	  const checks = loaded.catalog.checks;
   466	  const checkIds = checks.map((check) => check.id);
   467	  const known = new Map(checks.map((check) => [check.id, check]));
   468	  const supplied = Array.isArray(input?.rows) ? input.rows : [];
   469	  const grouped = new Map();
   470	  for (const row of supplied) {
   471	    const id = row?.check_id;
   472	    if (!known.has(id)) { errors.push(`unknown check ID: ${redactCapturedText(id ?? "<missing>")}`); continue; }
   473	    const list = grouped.get(id) || [];
   474	    list.push(row);
   475	    grouped.set(id, list);
   476	  }
   477	  const rows = checks.map((check) => {
   478	    const candidates = grouped.get(check.id) || [];
   479	    if (candidates.length === 0) {
   480	      errors.push(`${check.id}: missing ledger row`);
   481	      return missingRow(check);
   482	    }
   483	    const normalized = normalizeRow(candidates[0], check, inspected.artifact);
   484	    if (candidates.length > 1) {
   485	      normalized.validation_errors.push(`duplicate ledger rows: ${candidates.length}`);
   486	      errors.push(`${check.id}: duplicate ledger rows`);
   487	    }
   488	    if (normalized.validation_errors.length) errors.push(...normalized.validation_errors.map((error) => `${check.id}: ${error}`));
   489	    return normalized;
   490	  });
   491	  const evidenceHashesVerified = rows.every((row) => row.evidence && hashEvidence(row.evidence) === row.evidence.sha256);
   492	  const provenanceHashesVerified = rows.every((row) => row.provenance && hashProvenance(row.provenance) === row.provenance.sha256 && row.provenance.artifact_identity === inspected.artifact.identity && row.provenance.artifact_sha256 === inspected.artifact.sha256);
   493	  const catalogComplete = loaded.errors.length === 0 && new Set(checkIds).size === checkIds.length && rows.length === checkIds.length;
   494	  const blockers = rows.filter((row) => {
   495	    const check = known.get(row.check_id);
   496	    const validOptionalNa = row.status === "n/a" && check?.required === false && row.validation_errors.length === 0;
   497	    return !validOptionalNa && (row.status !== "pass" || row.validation_errors.length > 0);
   498	  }).map((row) => row.check_id);
   499	  if (!inspected.artifact.hash_verified) blockers.push("artifact");
   500	  if (errors.some((error) => error.startsWith("unknown check ID") || error.startsWith("catalog:") || error.startsWith("catalog path") || error.startsWith("input catalog"))) blockers.push("catalog");
   501	  if (!execution.allowed) blockers.push("execution");
   502	  const uniqueBlockers = [...new Set(blockers)];
   503	  const validation = {
   504	    input_valid: errors.length === 0,
   505	    execution_policy_valid: execution.allowed,
   506	    catalog_complete: catalogComplete,
   507	    artifact_hash_verified: inspected.artifact.hash_verified,
   508	    evidence_hashes_verified: evidenceHashesVerified,
   509	    provenance_hashes_verified: provenanceHashesVerified,
   510	    errors: [...new Set(errors)],
   511	  };
   512	  const releaseEligible = validation.input_valid && execution.allowed && catalogComplete && inspected.artifact.hash_verified && evidenceHashesVerified && provenanceHashesVerified && uniqueBlockers.length === 0;
   513	  return {
   514	    schema_version: SCHEMA_VERSION,
   515	    kind: KIND,
   516	    execution: execution.policy,
   517	    catalog: { path: CATALOG_PATH, sha256: loaded.sha256, check_ids: checkIds },
   518	    artifact: inspected.artifact,
   519	    rows,
   520	    verdict: releaseEligible ? "SHIP" : "HOLD",
   521	    release_eligible: releaseEligible,
   522	    blockers: uniqueBlockers,
   523	    validation,
   524	  };
   525	}
   526	
   527	function readInput(root, inputPath) {
   528	  const target = resolveRepoPath(root, inputPath);
   529	  return JSON.parse(readFileSync(target.absolute, "utf8"));
   530	}
   531	
   532	function resolveOutputPath(root, value) {
   533	  if (!nonempty(value) || value.startsWith("/") || /^[A-Za-z]:[\\/]/.test(value)) throw new Error("output path must be a non-empty repo-relative path");
   534	  const normalized = normalizeRelative(value);
   535	  if (normalized.split("/").includes("..")) throw new Error("output path may not contain '..'");
   536	  const absolute = resolve(root, normalized);
   537	  if (absolute !== root && !absolute.startsWith(`${root}${sep}`)) throw new Error("output path escapes repository root");
   538	  return absolute;
   539	}
   540	
   541	function cli() {
   542	  const args = process.argv.slice(2);
   543	  const inputIndex = args.indexOf("--input");
   544	  const outIndex = args.indexOf("--out");
   545	  const positional = args.filter((arg, index) => !arg.startsWith("--") && index !== inputIndex + 1 && index !== outIndex + 1);
   546	  const inputPath = inputIndex >= 0 ? args[inputIndex + 1] : positional[0];
   547	  const outPath = outIndex >= 0 ? args[outIndex + 1] : positional[1];
   548	  if (!inputPath || inputPath.startsWith("--")) {
   549	    console.error("Usage: node skills/tastecheck-pass/assets/release-gate.mjs --input <repo-relative-ledger.json> [--out <repo-relative-report.json>]");
   550	    process.exitCode = 2;
   551	    return;
   552	  }
   553	  let result;
   554	  try { result = evaluateReleaseGate(readInput(ROOT, inputPath), { root: ROOT }); }
   555	  catch (error) {
   556	    result = { schema_version: SCHEMA_VERSION, kind: KIND, catalog: { path: CATALOG_PATH, sha256: "0".repeat(64), check_ids: [] }, artifact: { type: "file", identity: "unresolved", sha256: "0".repeat(64), bytes: 0, hash_verified: false }, execution: { ...DEFAULT_EXECUTION }, rows: [], verdict: "HOLD", release_eligible: false, blockers: ["input"], validation: { input_valid: false, execution_policy_valid: true, catalog_complete: false, artifact_hash_verified: false, evidence_hashes_verified: false, provenance_hashes_verified: false, errors: [error.message] } };
   557	  }
   558	  const output = `${JSON.stringify(result, null, 2)}\n`;
   559	  if (outPath && !outPath.startsWith("--")) {
   560	    writeFileSync(resolveOutputPath(ROOT, outPath), output);
   561	  } else process.stdout.write(output);
   562	  if (result.verdict !== "SHIP") process.exitCode = 1;
   563	}
   564	
   565	if (process.argv[1] && resolve(process.argv[1]) === fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)) cli();

     1	#!/usr/bin/env node
     2	/* cdp-qa.mjs — zero-dependency tastecheck evidence driver.
     3	   Spawns system Chrome headless (temp profile), drives it over CDP via Node's
     4	   built-in WebSocket. NEVER touches the CEO's Chrome profile/session (browser-host law).
     5	   Usage: node cdp-qa.mjs <file-url> <out-dir>
     6	   Evidence: cold-load gate audit, keyboard tab trace, reflow checks, contrast samples,
     7	   AX-tree summary, radio/export path, screenshots (light/dark/narrow). */
     8	import { spawn } from "node:child_process";
     9	import { readFileSync, writeFileSync, mkdirSync, existsSync } from "node:fs";
    10	
    11	const CHROME = "/Applications/Google Chrome.app/Contents/MacOS/Google Chrome";
    12	const TARGET = process.argv[2];
    13	const OUT = process.argv[3] || "/tmp/p1qa-evidence";
    14	mkdirSync(OUT, { recursive: true });
    15	const E = {}; // evidence bag
    16	
    17	const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
    18	const chrome = spawn(CHROME, [
    19	  "--headless", "--disable-gpu", "--no-first-run", "--no-default-browser-check",
    20	  "--remote-debugging-port=0", "--user-data-dir=" + OUT + "/profile",
    21	  "--window-size=1440,900", "about:blank",
    22	], { stdio: "ignore" });
    23	
    24	async function waitPort(file, tries = 60) {
    25	  for (let i = 0; i < tries; i++) {
    26	    try { return readFileSync(file, "utf8").split("\n")[0]; } catch { await sleep(250); }
    27	  }
    28	  throw new Error("DevToolsActivePort never appeared");
    29	}
    30	const port = await waitPort(OUT + "/profile/DevToolsActivePort");
    31	
    32	// fetch page target ws url
    33	const list = await (await fetch(`http://127.0.0.1:${port}/json/list`)).json();
    34	const page = list.find((t) => t.type === "page");
    35	const ws = new WebSocket(page.webSocketDebuggerUrl);
    36	await new Promise((res, rej) => { ws.onopen = res; ws.onerror = rej; });
    37	
    38	let msgId = 0; const pending = new Map(); const events = [];
    39	ws.onmessage = (ev) => {
    40	  const m = JSON.parse(ev.data);
    41	  if (m.id && pending.has(m.id)) { pending.get(m.id)(m); pending.delete(m.id); }
    42	  else if (m.method) events.push(m);
    43	};
    44	function send(method, params = {}) {
    45	  return new Promise((res) => {
    46	    const id = ++msgId; pending.set(id, res);
    47	    ws.send(JSON.stringify({ id, method, params }));
    48	  });
    49	}
    50	async function evaluate(expr) {
    51	  const r = await send("Runtime.evaluate", { expression: expr, returnByValue: true, awaitPromise: true });
    52	  if (r.result && r.result.exceptionDetails) throw new Error("eval: " + JSON.stringify(r.result.exceptionDetails).slice(0, 300));
    53	  return r.result && r.result.result ? r.result.result.value : undefined;
    54	}
    55	
    56	// ---------- 1. cold load ----------
    57	await send("Page.enable"); await send("Runtime.enable");
    58	await send("Emulation.setEmulatedMedia", { features: [{ name: "prefers-color-scheme", value: "light" }] });
    59	await send("Page.navigate", { url: TARGET });
    60	await sleep(2500);
    61	E.coldLoad = await evaluate(`(() => {
    62	  const doc = document.querySelector("#docview article h1");
    63	  return { title: document.title, docOpen: doc ? doc.textContent : null,
    64	    docBtns: document.querySelectorAll(".dlink").length,
    65	    groups: document.querySelectorAll(".grptitle").length,
    66	    cur: document.querySelector('.dlink[aria-current="true"]') ? true : false,
    67	    hasMain: !!document.querySelector("main#main"), hasSkip: !!document.querySelector("a.skip"),
    68	    hasNav: !!document.querySelector("aside[aria-label]"), bodyText: document.body.innerText.length };
    69	})()`);
    70	
    71	// ---------- 2. gate-audit.js (fresh load, before interactions) ----------
    72	const auditSrc = readFileSync(new URL("./gate-audit.js", import.meta.url), "utf8");
    73	E.gateAudit = await evaluate(`(() => {
    74	  window.__cap = []; ["log","warn","error"].forEach(k => {
    75	    const orig = console[k].bind(console); console[k] = (...a) => { window.__cap.push(k + ": " + a.join(" ")); orig(...a); };
    76	  });
    77	  try { ${auditSrc} gateAudit(); } catch (e) { window.__cap.push("error: threw " + e.message); }
    78	  return window.__cap;
    79	})()`);
    80	
    81	// ---------- 3. keyboard tab trace ----------
    82	const focusLog = [];
    83	for (let i = 0; i < 14; i++) {
    84	  await send("Input.dispatchKeyEvent", { type: "keyDown", key: "Tab", code: "Tab", windowsVirtualKeyCode: 9 });
    85	  await send("Input.dispatchKeyEvent", { type: "keyUp", key: "Tab", code: "Tab", windowsVirtualKeyCode: 9 });
    86	  focusLog.push(await evaluate(`(() => { const e = document.activeElement; if (!e || e === document.body) return "body";
    87	    const s = getComputedStyle(e); return e.tagName.toLowerCase() + (e.id ? "#" + e.id : "") +
    88	      (e.className && typeof e.className === "string" ? "." + e.className.split(" ")[0] : "") +
    89	      " | outline:" + s.outlineStyle + " " + s.outlineWidth + " " + s.outlineColor; })()`));
    90	}
    91	E.tabTrace = focusLog;
    92	
    93	// ---------- 4. reflow: 320px + 200% text-ish (overflow scan) ----------
    94	async function overflowAt(width) {
    95	  await send("Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride", { width, height: 1400, deviceScaleFactor: 1, mobile: false });
    96	  await sleep(400);
    97	  return evaluate(`(() => {
    98	    const bad = [];
    99	    document.querySelectorAll("body *").forEach(el => {
   100	      const r = el.getBoundingClientRect();
   101	      if (r.right > document.documentElement.clientWidth + 2 && getComputedStyle(el).position !== "fixed") {
   102	        const t = (el.textContent || "").replace(/\\s+/g, " ").trim().slice(0, 30);
   103	        bad.push(el.tagName.toLowerCase() + (el.className && typeof el.className === "string" ? "." + el.className.split(" ")[0] : "") + " right=" + Math.round(r.right) + (t ? " «" + t + "»" : ""));
   104	      }
   105	    });
   106	    return { vw: document.documentElement.clientWidth, scrollW: document.documentElement.scrollWidth, overflowEls: bad.slice(0, 6) };
   107	  })()`);
   108	}
   109	E.reflow320 = await overflowAt(320);
   110	E.reflowNarrow = await overflowAt(390);
   111	await send("Emulation.clearDeviceMetricsOverride");
   112	
   113	// ---------- 5. contrast sampling (computed, rendered nodes, light) ----------
   114	E.contrast = await evaluate(`(() => {
   115	  const sel = [["body","ink/paper"], [".muted","muted"], ["a[data-internal]","link"], ["article a","link2"],
   116	    [".chip","chip"], [".chip.review","chip-review"], [".btn.primary","btn-primary"], [".masthead h1","head"],
   117	    [".gate","gate"], [".csum b","csum"], [".dq","dq"]];
   118	  const out = [];
   119	  sel.forEach(([s, n]) => { const el = document.querySelector(s); if (!el) return;
   120	    const c = getComputedStyle(el); let bg = c.backgroundColor, txt = c.color;
   121	    let p = el.parentElement; while (bg === "rgba(0, 0, 0, 0)" && p) { bg = getComputedStyle(p).backgroundColor; p = p.parentElement; }
   122	    out.push({ n, fg: txt, bg, font: c.fontSize }); });
   123	  return out;
   124	})()`);
   125	
   126	// ---------- 6. curate path: open, radio, note, storage, export ----------
   127	await evaluate(`document.querySelector('.viewbtn[data-view="curate"]').click()`);
   128	await sleep(400);
   129	E.curate = await evaluate(`(() => {
   130	  const cards = document.querySelectorAll(".dcard").length;
   131	  const radios = document.querySelectorAll('input[type="radio"]').length;
   132	  const first = document.querySelector(".dcard");
   133	  const legendOk = !!first && !!first.querySelector("legend");
   134	  return { cards, radios, legendOk, h1: document.querySelector(".curhead h1") ? document.querySelector(".curhead h1").textContent : null };
   135	})()`);
   136	// radio via keyboard: focus first radio then arrow? use real click-equivalent: dispatch space after focus
   137	await evaluate(`(() => { const r = document.querySelector('.dcard input[value="approve"]'); r.focus(); return document.activeElement === r; })()`);
   138	await send("Input.dispatchKeyEvent", { type: "keyDown", key: " ", code: "Space", windowsVirtualKeyCode: 32 });
   139	await send("Input.dispatchKeyEvent", { type: "keyUp", key: " ", code: "Space", windowsVirtualKeyCode: 32 });
   140	await sleep(200);
   141	E.radio = await evaluate(`(() => { const d = document.querySelector(".dcard"); return { dataSt: d.getAttribute("data-st"),
   142	  ls: JSON.parse(localStorage.getItem("p1-curation-v1") || "{}").decisions || {} }; })()`);
   143	await send("Browser.setDownloadBehavior", { behavior: "allow", downloadPath: OUT, eventsEnabled: true });
   144	E.export = await evaluate(`(() => { const before = document.querySelectorAll("a[download]").length;
   145	  document.getElementById("exp").click(); return { before, toast: (document.getElementById("status").textContent || "") }; })()`);
   146	await sleep(1500);
   147	const fs = await import("node:fs");
   148	E.downloadFiles = fs.readdirSync(OUT).filter((f) => f.endsWith(".json"));
   149	// reset local state
   150	await evaluate(`localStorage.removeItem("p1-curation-v1")`);
   151	
   152	// ---------- 7. AX tree summary ----------
   153	await send("Accessibility.enable");
   154	const ax = await send("Accessibility.getFullAXTree");
   155	const roles = {};
   156	(ax.result.nodes || []).forEach((n) => { roles[n.role && n.role.value] = (roles[n.role && n.role.value] || 0) + 1; });
   157	const names = (ax.result.nodes || []).filter((n) => ["banner", "navigation", "main", "contentinfo", "heading", "radio"].includes(n.role && n.role.value)).slice(0, 30)
   158	  .map((n) => (n.role ? n.role.value : "?") + ":" + ((n.name && n.name.value) || "").slice(0, 40));
   159	E.ax = { roles, sample: names };
   160	
   161	// ---------- 8. screenshots ----------
   162	async function shot(name, features) {
   163	  await send("Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride", { width: 1440, height: 1800, deviceScaleFactor: 1, mobile: false });
   164	  await send("Emulation.setEmulatedMedia", { features });
   165	  await sleep(500);
   166	  await evaluate(`document.querySelector('.dlink[data-id="DIGEST-CEO-2026-09-03.md"]').click()`);
   167	  await sleep(500);
   168	  const s = await send("Page.captureScreenshot", { format: "png" });
   169	  writeFileSync(OUT + "/shot-" + name + ".png", Buffer.from(s.result.data, "base64"));
   170	  await evaluate(`document.querySelector('.viewbtn[data-view="curate"]').click()`);
   171	  await sleep(300);
   172	  const s2 = await send("Page.captureScreenshot", { format: "png" });
   173	  writeFileSync(OUT + "/shot-" + name + "-curate.png", Buffer.from(s2.result.data, "base64"));
   174	}
   175	await shot("light", [{ name: "prefers-color-scheme", value: "light" }]);
   176	await shot("dark", [{ name: "prefers-color-scheme", value: "dark" }]);
   177	
   178	writeFileSync(OUT + "/evidence.json", JSON.stringify(E, null, 2));
   179	console.log("EVIDENCE WRITTEN:", OUT + "/evidence.json");
   180	try { chrome.kill(); } catch {}
   181	process.exit(0);

    76	
    77	function makeEvidence(check, status = "pass", overrides = {}) {
    78	  const mode = check.manual_inspector_required ? "manual" : "automated";
    79	  const evidence = {
    80	    mode,
    81	    summary: `${check.label} evidence for ${status}`,
    82	    details: { check_id: check.id, observed: status === "pass" },
    83	    ...(status === "n/a" ? { subject: check.applicability.subject, subject_absent: true } : {}),
    84	    ...overrides,
    85	  };
    86	  evidence.sha256 = hashEvidence(evidence);
    87	  return evidence;
    88	}
    89	
    90	function makeRow(check, status = "pass", evidenceOverrides = {}, provenanceOverrides = {}) {
    91	  const evidence = makeEvidence(check, status, evidenceOverrides);
    92	  const provenance = {
    93	    artifact_identity: measuredArtifact.identity,
    94	    artifact_sha256: measuredArtifact.sha256,
    95	    captured_at: capturedAt,
    96	    tool: { name: "tastecheck-test-runner", version: "1.0.0" },
    97	    browser: { name: "Chromium", version: "126.0.0" },
    98	    inspector: check.manual_inspector_required ? { name: "fixture-reviewer", role: "auditor", method: "browser fixture" } : null,
    99	    ...provenanceOverrides,
   100	  };
   101	  provenance.sha256 = hashProvenance(provenance);
   102	  return {
   103	    skill: "tastecheck-pass",
   104	    check_id: check.id,
   105	    status,
   106	    reason: `${check.label} was checked on the committed fixture.`,
   107	    remediation: `Rerun ${check.id} after the next artifact change.`,
   108	    evidence,
   109	    provenance,
   110	    ...(check.judgment === "subjective" ? { review: makeReview(check, status) } : {}),
   111	  };
   112	}
   113	
   114	function makeReview(check, status = "pass", overrides = {}) {
   115	  const review = {
   116	    reviewer: { id: "human-reviewer-1", type: "human", role: "independent-auditor", method: "rubric review" },
   117	    rubric: { id: `rubric-${check.stage}`, version: "1.0", criteria: { evidence_bound: true, decision_bound: true } },
   118	    independent: true,
   119	    decision: status,
   120	    disagreement: false,
   121	    adjudication: null,
   122	    reviewed_at: capturedAt,
   123	    ...overrides,
   124	  };
   125	  review.sha256 = hashReview(review);
   126	  return review;
   127	}

     1	---
     2	name: deslop-ui
     3	description: >-
     4	  Anti-slop UI review for generated or generic frontend work. Use after LLM UI
     5	  output, or for requests to remove AI tells: purple gradients, pill CTAs,
     6	  default type, centered heroes, three-card grids, glassmorphism, and template sameness.
     7	---
     8	
     9	# Deslop UI
    10	
    11	Slop is predictable, uncommitted design—not merely ugly design. Audit observable
    12	surface, structure, and voice against a brief; repair with a specific, verifiable choice.
    13	
    14	## Repair order and evidence contract
    15	
    16	1. Establish brief, preserved signals, and artifact; record surface, structural, and verbal findings separately.
    17	2. Cite a selector, section, phrase, screenshot, or browser observation; assign P0–P3.
    18	3. Choose a brief-bound named direction, prescribe repairs, and reinspect the same views.
    19	
    20	`n/a` requires an absent subject. Stop before changing a coherent brief-supported brand
    21	choice; ask for direction when the brief or acceptance criteria are absent.
    22	
    23	## Diagnose cause, then tell
    24	
    25	Make two passes: **intent** (job, audience, hierarchy, retained signals, claimed
    26	signature) and **behavior** (rendered first view, section transitions, narrow view;
    27	trace code to selector/token or name screenshot geometry). Then ask whether replacing
    28	the suspect with a neutral version changes meaning or task performance. Preserve a
    29	documented functional pill, gradient, table, or system font; repair its implementation,
    30	not its role.
    31	
    32	Rank P0 meaning/interaction/accessibility/fact, P1 direction/hierarchy, P2 repeated
    33	default, then P3 polish. Every P0–P2 packet names: subject; observation plus brief;
    34	failure mechanism; preserved signal; concrete composition/color/type/shape/voice
    35	replacement and owner; after-state observation (including narrow view). Make at least
    36	one repair at the causal structural level when the skeleton is generic.
    37	
    38	## Fast audit and self-check
    39	
    40	Render before acting; grep is only a lead. Check these detector-to-decision pairs:
    41	
    42	| Plane | Detector | Required repair decision |
    43	|---|---|---|
    44	| surface | unsupported purple/white gradient, default neutral tokens, equal-weight palette | dominant field, purposeful accent, semantic tokens, contrast |
    45	| type/shape | unexamined default type, timid hierarchy, pill CTA, universal radius/shadow | type stance and role-based scale/radius/elevation |
    46	| structure | centered hero → one CTA → equal cards, uniform section rhythm | replacement topology, lead/supporting evidence, narrow reading order |
    47	| decoration/function | default glass/blobs/emoji/gradient text, static forms, placeholder copy | brief-supported atmosphere or none; route behavior/copy to the owning skill |
    48	
    49	Every finding needs subject, P0–P3, observed evidence, preserved signal, replacement,
    50	and after-state proof. Verify all three planes: surface; structural section stack and
    51	rhythm; verbal claims (hand factual prose to `humanize-copy`). A named direction must
    52	state a concrete aesthetic, dominant field/accident job, type stance, and signature
    53	move. Re-run the original views after repair.
    54	
    55	## Reference files
    56	
    57	- `references/anti-patterns.md` — full detector catalog and fixes.
    58	- `references/structural-tells.md` — structural audit and dated attractors.
    59	- `references/design-direction.md` — positive direction method.
    60	- `references/decision-records.md` — novel-case ADR rules.
    61	
    62	## How to deliver
    63	
    64	Deliver a prioritized repair specification and named direction; say what changed, why,
    65	what survives, and how the after-state proves it. Respect existing tokens and pair with
    66	`web-typography`, `color-system`, and `micro-motion` as needed.
    67	
    68	<!-- contract:v1:start -->
    69	## Contract (generated)
    70	
    71	Canonical detail: [contract.json](contract.json).
    72	
    73	- Route: A rendered or coded interface feels generic and needs a structural, visual, and verbal anti-slop audit. (+1 in contract.json); avoid: The task is a greenfield aesthetic interview before a page exists. (+1 in contract.json)
    74	- Exclude: Do not replace a coherent brand system merely because it is not your preference. (+2 in contract.json)
    75	- Stop / handoff: Stop before changing a coherent branded choice that is supported by the brief. (+2 in contract.json); receives [design-system-interview, improve-existing-website, art-direction, responsive-layout] -> sends [web-typography, color-system, humanize-copy, tastecheck-pass, a11y-pass, empty-states, form-ux, micro-motion]
    76	- Output: prioritized anti-slop repair specification and committed direction
    77	- Evidence: `table_with_evidence` with `status`, `reason`, `remediation`, `evidence`, `provenance`.

     1	---
     2	name: design-system-interview
     3	description: >-
     4	  Design-system interview before new frontend builds or redesigns. Use for vague
     5	  site/app/landing/dashboard requests, generic-looking UI, direction-setting,
     6	  aesthetic choices, type/color/density decisions, and DESIGN-SYSTEM.md tokens.
     7	---
     8	
     9	# Design System Interview
    10	
    11	Turn a vague frontend brief into decisions a builder can use. The interview should feel
    12	like a sharp creative director at the table: it studies the evidence, recommends a point
    13	of view, and asks only questions that materially change the build.
    14	
    15	## Start with a direction, not a questionnaire
    16	
    17	Inspect the product job, audience, content, existing marks, constraints, locales, and any
    18	visual references before asking a question. Then open with:
    19	
    20	```markdown
    21	What I see: <the strongest signal in the brief>
    22	My recommendation: <a specific direction>
    23	Why it fits: <the product consequence>
    24	Choose: <fork A and trade-off> / <fork B and trade-off> / redirect me
    25	```
    26	
    27	Ask one high-consequence fork at a time. Combine questions only when one answer genuinely
    28	settles several decisions. After each answer, reflect the decision back in one line so the
    29	user can correct it without rereading the conversation.
    30	
    31	## The interview loop
    32	
    33	1. **Read the room.** Separate current evidence from historical residue. If an existing
    34	   direction already covers five dimensions, confirm it and ask only for the gaps.
    35	2. **Propose a fork.** Give two brief-compatible outcomes that would look or behave
    36	   materially differently. Recommend one and name its trade-off.
    37	3. **Turn language into consequences.** Translate “clean,” “premium,” or “bold” into
    38	   hierarchy, density, material, type, color, or rhythm. Do not debate adjectives.
    39	4. **Record the decision.** Mark it `committed`, `assumption awaiting confirmation`, or
    40	   `blocked by contradiction`, with the evidence that earned it.
    41	5. **Stop when the system is buildable.** All nine dimensions are decided, explicitly
    42	   delegated to the recommendation, or blocked. Then write the artifact and handoff.
    43	
    44	Use 4–10 exchanges for a full interview. For urgent internal work, offer the recommended
    45	direction as a short approval pass. In a one-shot or interrupted run, save the decision
    46	snapshot and name the first question needed to resume; do not present assumptions as approval.
    47	
    48	## Decisions to close
    49	
    50	Use these adaptively; `references/interview-contract.generated.md` is the canonical
    51	session/dimension authority. Examples teach format, never taste.
    52	
    53	| Canonical ID | Close with |
    54	| --- | --- |
    55	| `reference` | a real artifact and what it earns |
    56	| `personality` | a chosen pole, not a middle |
    57	| `aesthetic` | one concrete phrase predicting hierarchy/material |
    58	| `type` | binding evidence, display/body stance, language/measure risk |
    59	| `color_mode` | dominant hue, accent job, and light/dark commitment |
    60	| `density_shape` | density, numeric radius range, elevation |
    61	| `structure_rhythm` | composition, motif, and sectional cadence |
    62	| `signature` | one memorable move |
    63	| `imagery_iconography` | source/treatment or absence; one icon system |
    64	
    65	Optionally set motion level for products.
    66	
    67	## What the user receives
    68	
    69	Write `DESIGN-SYSTEM.md` from `assets/DESIGN-SYSTEM.template.md`, then give the user a
    70	short handoff they can approve or build from immediately:
    71	
    72	1. **Direction:** one sentence that predicts the interface.
    73	2. **Decisions:** the completed decision map.
    74	3. **Refusals:** three to five defaults this product will not use.
    75	4. **Build contract:** semantic tokens, structural rhythm, and responsive/accessibility constraints.
    76	5. **Next move:** the first implementation step, or the exact blocker and owner.
    77	
    78	Read `references/session-protocol.md` for readiness and resume rules.
    79	
    80	```markdown
    81	| Dimension / conflict | Evidence | Decision or assumption | Consequence | Confirmation / owner |
    82	| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
    83	| Alert hierarchy | ... | committed / assumed / blocked | ... | ... |
    84	```
    85	
    86	Use `approved` only when the direction is confirmed and buildable. Use `approval-ready`
    87	when recommendations and tokens are complete but await confirmation; do not hand that
    88	state to implementation. Use `blocked` when a contradiction or missing authority prevents
    89	a safe recommendation. Each non-obvious choice cites its source; each token has a real build job.
    90	
    91	```
    92	The committed direction in one line: "<source-derived aesthetic phrase> — <chosen
    93	hierarchy>, <color role>, <type stance>, <shape/density>, signature = <specific move>."
    94	```
    95	
    96	Hand off hue to `color-system`, type to `web-typography`, modes to `theming`, tokens to
    97	implementation skills, and the completed spec to `deslop-ui` for audit. Preserve a
    98	single `DESIGN-SYSTEM.md` as the source of truth.
    99	
   100	## Reference files
   101	
   102	- `references/interview-bank.md` — forks and abstention guidance; read while interviewing.
   103	- `references/structure-and-rhythm.md` — composition and rhythm; read before committing structure.
   104	- `references/tokens.md` — token architecture; read when writing the artifact.
   105	- `references/decision-records.md` — novel cases.
   106	- `references/session-protocol.md` — shortcut, headless, contradiction, and resume states.
   107	- `references/interview-contract.generated.md` — generated session and dimension authority.
   108	
   109	## Ready-to-build check
   110	
   111	Report these rows with direct evidence, reason, remediation, and `pass`/`fail`/`n/a`.
   112	
   113	| Check | Status | Evidence / provenance | Reason | Remediation |
   114	| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
   115	| Nine required dimensions decided |  |  |  |  |
   116	| Existing-direction shortcut or full interview justified |  |  |  |  |
   117	| Contradictions and trust-critical rationale resolved |  |  |  |  |
   118	| DESIGN-SYSTEM.md and canonical token block complete |  |  |  |  |
   119	| Downstream handoff is explicit |  |  |  |  |
   120	
   121	The interview is build-ready only when every row passes and the artifact is `approved`.
   122	An `approval-ready` or `blocked` output is a resumable checkpoint: name the unresolved
   123	owner and next confirmation, but do not start implementation. Deliver the one-line
   124	direction first, link the artifact, then state the next allowed action.
   125

     1	---
     2	name: improve-existing-website
     3	description: >-
     4	  Use when improving an existing website or codebase while retaining its recognizable
     5	  identity, especially when evidence, scope, or redesign approval is uncertain.
     6	---
     7	
     8	# Improve Existing Website
     9	
    10	Improve what is weak without sanding away what makes the product recognizable. Existing
    11	work contains both signal and drift; evidence decides which is which.
    12	
    13	## 1. Inspect the product people actually use
    14	
    15	Open the rendered priority path before prescribing. Trace entry, decision, action, and
    16	recovery at wide and narrow widths. Then inspect source hierarchy, tokens, repeated
    17	components, content, analytics or user evidence supplied in scope, and business cues.
    18	
    19	Label what you can point to **EVIDENCE**. Label every interpretation **INFERRED**. A direct
    20	observation is “the primary action changes label across three screens”; “the brand values
    21	warmth” is inferred unless the brief or product evidence says so.
    22	
    23	**Done when:** the priority path, recurring system choices, and highest-cost failures are
    24	observable rather than guessed.
    25	
    26	## 2. Separate identity from drift
    27	
    28	Record signals worth preserving: distinctive type, color relationships, interaction
    29	patterns, content voice, layout logic, or customer-recognizable assets. Classify each
    30	candidate change:
    31	
    32	| Class | Meaning | Authority |
    33	| --- | --- | --- |
    34	| **Preserve** | coherent, repeated, brief-supported identity | repair defects without replacing the signal |
    35	| **Normalize** | accidental variation against an established pattern | safe to align when evidence is strong |
    36	| **Approval needed** | a new hierarchy, palette, voice, or material redesign | show the choice and wait for owner agreement |
    37	
    38	Use `references/signal-vs-drift.md` when repetition, age, or inconsistency could plausibly
    39	be intentional. Age alone is not evidence of drift.
    40	
    41	**Done when:** every proposed change names what survives and why the change is authorized.
    42	
    43	## 3. Form the smallest coherent direction
    44	
    45	Complete `assets/INFERRED-SYSTEM.template.md` across audience, territory, type, color,
    46	layout, components, voice, and accessibility. Score readiness with
    47	`references/inspection-state-machine.md`. Ask only questions whose answers would change
    48	the safe direction; otherwise state the inference and its evidence.
    49	
    50	Do not solve a system problem with isolated polish. Choose a narrow vertical slice that
    51	proves the direction across a real path—for example navigation, one content section, one
    52	form or state, and the narrow layout—before propagating it.
    53	
    54	**Done when:** the direction can be approved, rejected, or resumed without reconstructing
    55	the investigation.
    56	
    57	## 4. Approve, repair, and verify
    58	
    59	For each repair, record the observed failure, preserved signal, smallest coherent edit,
    60	owner or approval source, and proof. After approval, use
    61	`references/repair-handoffs.md` for evidence-justified specialist passes. Verify the same
    62	path and viewports used during inspection; comparison must show that the defect changed
    63	and the preserved signal survived.
    64	
    65	If work pauses, save the current state, unresolved question, evidence, and exact next
    66	decision using `references/inspection-state-machine.md`. Resumption starts from that
    67	checkpoint, not from a fresh aesthetic opinion.
    68	
    69	**Done when:** the implementation report distinguishes repaired drift, preserved identity,
    70	browser/static proof, and deliberately deferred ambiguity.
    71	
    72	## Reference files
    73	
    74	- `references/audit-procedure.md` — extraction order.
    75	- `references/signal-vs-drift.md` — identity versus accident.
    76	- `references/decision-records.md` — scope ADR rules.
    77	- `references/inspection-state-machine.md` — readiness and approval.
    78	- `assets/improve-checklist.md` — completion ledger.
    79	- `references/repair-handoffs.md` — routing and skip rules.
    80	- `references/interview-contract.generated.md` — canonical state/question authority.
    81	
    82	## Self-check
    83	
    84	`assets/improve-checklist.md` is the completion authority for inspection, labels,
    85	approval, handoffs, and evidence.
    86

     1	---
     2	name: color-system
     3	description: >-
     4	  OKLCH color-system guidance for cohesive palettes and contrast-safe tokens. Use
     5	  when choosing colors, generating ramps, fixing muddy palettes, creating semantic
     6	  state colors, checking WCAG contrast, or defining theme variables.
     7	---
     8	
     9	# Color System
    10	
    11	Build a palette whose roles survive real text, controls, themes, and gamut limits.
    12	OKLCH makes lightness and chroma easier to reason about than HSL or hex, but it does
    13	not choose the art direction or guarantee contrast. The rendered pair remains the
    14	test.
    15	
    16	## Why OKLCH
    17	
    18	`oklch(L C H)`: **L** 0–1 perceptual lightness, **C** chroma (0 = gray, ~0.37 max
    19	real), **H** 0–360 hue. Benefits that matter:
    20	- Similar L values are a useful starting point for visually even ramps; hue and chroma
    21	  still affect the result, so inspect and measure it.
    22	- Adjust one property without wrecking the others (lighten without desaturating).
    23	- Wide-gamut (P3) ready; degrades to sRGB. Supported in all current evergreen browsers;
    24	  for very old targets provide a hex fallback (build tools can emit both).
    25	
    26	## The method
    27	
    28	1. **Choose a palette structure from evidence.** A restrained product may need one hue
    29	   and neutrals; a data or editorial system may need several. Record what each hue does.
    30	2. **Build a lightness ramp** at fixed steps. A 10–12 stop ramp from ~0.97 (50) down
    31	   to ~0.30 (900). Keep H constant; vary L; let C peak in the mid-tones and taper at
    32	   the extremes (very light and very dark can't hold high chroma).
    33	3. **Choose neutral temperature deliberately.** Low-chroma brand tinting can create
    34	   cohesion; true neutrals can be better for data, photography, or strict status colors.
    35	4. **Pick semantic colors** (success/error/warning/info) at matched L/C so they belong
    36	   to the same family (don't paste in a random stock red/green).
    37	5. **Derive interaction states** (hover/active) by nudging L ±0.04–0.06, not by adding
    38	   opacity.
    39	6. **Verify contrast** for every text/bg pair you'll actually use.
    40	
    41	## Derive from the brief, not this file
    42	
    43	The sample demonstrates ramp mechanics, not a palette menu. Start from brand evidence,
    44	audience, ambient light, and semantic roles; state why hue, neutral temperature, and
    45	accent energy fit. If evidence is missing, record the approved assumption rather than
    46	defaulting to fashionable blue, violet, or “accessible” green.
    47	
    48	## Build a token system, not a swatch sheet
    49	
    50	Keep three layers separate: **primitives** are named OKLCH stops; **semantic roles**
    51	name jobs such as text, surface, focus, and danger; **component aliases** reference one
    52	role, never a raw value. Define a focus role, non-color status cue, and rendered
    53	foreground/background pair for each semantic status.
    54	
    55	## Non-negotiables
    56	
    57	- **Build ramps in OKLCH with controlled lightness and chroma.** Keep hue stable when
    58	  continuity matters; allow documented hue correction when a nominally constant ramp
    59	  looks uneven or leaves the target gamut.
    60	- **Give every hue a job.** Do not add equally weighted colors just to make the palette
    61	  feel complete; do not force a single-accent formula on evidence that needs more.
    62	- **Choose neutral temperature from the content.** Tinted and true-neutral ramps are
    63	  both valid when their role is explicit.
    64	- **Verify WCAG on real pairs:** body text ≥ 4.5:1, large text/UI/icons ≥ 3:1. A color
    65	  that looks fine can still fail; measure. WCAG 2.x ratios are the AA conformance
    66	  thresholds used here; legal applicability depends on the project's jurisdiction.
    67	  Treat APCA as an additional reading check, not a substitute for the required gate.
    68	  CSS `contrast-color()` is useful where supported but still ships only a black/white
    69	  answer — don't lean on it for brand pairs.
    70	- **Don't convey meaning by color alone** (color-blind users) — pair with icon/text.
    71	- **Chroma tapers at lightness extremes.** Max chroma lives in the mid L range; near-
    72	  white and near-black stops must drop C or they look neon/muddy.
    73	
    74	## Measure the rendered pair, including gamut and state
    75	
    76	Measure each rendered text, icon/UI, focus, and error pair in every supported theme and
    77	state; a passing body pair does not prove disabled or hover. For P3, retain hierarchy in
    78	the sRGB fallback and record gamut compression. If a brand hue cannot carry a legible
    79	role, use it as a mark/large accent and assign a darker semantic ink.
    80	
    81	## Quick-start
    82	
    83	Use `assets/oklch-ramp.md` for the generator and the semantic-token skeleton. Keep
    84	primitives separate from semantic roles; measure real text/UI pairs and emit hex
    85	fallbacks when legacy targets are in scope.
    86	
    87	## Reference files
    88	
    89	- `references/oklch-and-ramps.md` — OKLCH in depth, ramp math, chroma-taper, harmony
    90	  schemes, neutral tinting, P3/fallbacks. Read when generating a palette.
    91	- `references/contrast-and-tokens.md` — WCAG targets, measuring contrast, semantic
    92	  token architecture, interaction states, color-blind safety, light/dark sharing.
    93	- `references/decision-records.md` — meta-patterns + ADR rules for novel cases.
    94	
    95	## How to deliver
    96	
    97	State the brief-derived hue/neutral strategy, semantic tokens, measured pairs, gamut
    98	fallbacks, and unresolved gaps; hand role mappings to `theming`.
    99	
   100	## Completion evidence
   101	
   102	Record brief fit, primitive/role map, real contrast, non-color cues/gamut fallback, and
   103	theming handoff with evidence, reason, and remediation.
   104

     1	---
     2	name: web-typography
     3	description: >-
     4	  Use when a web product needs a contextual type system, resilient font loading,
     5	  multilingual glyph or wrap coverage, or readable hierarchy across operational and
     6	  editorial surfaces.
     7	---
     8	
     9	# Web Typography
    10	
    11	You are setting type for screens, not print. The goal is text that is **legible**
    12	(can you tell the letters apart), **readable** (is it comfortable to read at
    13	length), and **clear in hierarchy** (does the eye know where to go) — across every
    14	viewport, zoom level, and font preference a real user might have.
    15	
    16	Work top-down: establish roles, readable measure, scale, and loading behavior before
    17	polish. The result should suit the reading task, language coverage, density, and brand.
    18	
    19	## The decision order
    20	
    21	Make these decisions in sequence. Each one constrains the next.
    22	
    23	1. **Font choice** — For operational/product UI, a fast, good system stack is a
    24	   valid deliberate choice. For brand, marketing, portfolio, or launch surfaces,
    25	   start from the design-system interview and choose a display/body stance with
    26	   personality. If you load a web font, plan its loading (step 6) at the same time.
    27	2. **Base size & measure** — Start body text around `1rem` and sustained reading around
    28	   45–75 characters per line, then test the actual face, language, density, and viewport.
    29	3. **Type scale** — Define a small role-based scale. A modular ratio can generate a
    30	   starting point; optical hierarchy decides the final values. Use fluid sizing only
    31	   where the role benefits from it.
    32	4. **Vertical rhythm** — Start body line-height around 1.4–1.6 and tighten display
    33	   roles; verify clipping and spacing with the chosen face and scripts.
    34	5. **Hierarchy & polish** — Weight/size/space contrast, `text-wrap: balance` on
    35	   headings and `pretty` on body where supported, OpenType features, and optical
    36	   tracking. Treat numeric tracking recipes as starting points, not universal values.
    37	6. **Loading & performance** — choose `font-display`, preload only a genuinely critical
    38	   known resource, prefer WOFF2, and metric-match the fallback where reflow matters.
    39	7. **Accessibility pass** — Contrast, zoom to 200%, the WCAG 1.4.12 text-spacing
    40	   overrides, enabled zoom, and rendered review of any justified reading text.
    41	
    42	## Derive a type stance from use, not a sample pairing
    43	
    44	The quick-start faces and scales demonstrate constraints; they are not default answers.
    45	Start from the reading task, information density, language coverage, brand direction,
    46	and loading budget. A public narrative, a data-dense operational surface, and a
    47	multilingual transactional flow may all require different hierarchy and fallback
    48	decisions. Record the intended reading behavior, then show computed-size, wrap/measure,
    49	glyph-coverage, and loading evidence. Variation is meaningful only when it changes the
    50	type system’s structure, territory, and explanatory approach without weakening those
    51	checks.
    52	
    53	## Non-negotiables (the rules that prevent the common failures)
    54	
    55	These are the mistakes that show up again and again. Internalize the *why* so you
    56	can apply them in novel situations, not just copy them.
    57	
    58	- **Use user-relative units for the type system.** Prefer `rem` for document-level
    59	  roles and `em` where a component should scale with its context. A pixel declaration
    60	  is not automatically inaccessible, but a pixel-only system is easier to detach from
    61	  user preferences and harder to scale coherently.
    62	- **Line-height is unitless.** `line-height: 1.5` is inherited as a *ratio*, so
    63	  each element recomputes from its own size. A fixed `line-height: 24px` inherits
    64	  the computed pixel value and breaks on differently-sized children.
    65	- **Do not size text with unbounded viewport units.** For fluid display type, bound
    66	  the range and include a relative-unit contribution, then test browser zoom and text
    67	  resizing: `clamp(2rem, 1rem + 3vw, 4rem)`.
    68	- **Constrain the measure.** Lines longer than ~75 characters make the eye lose its
    69	  place on the return sweep; shorter than ~45 gets choppy. `max-width: 66ch` on
    70	  text containers is the single highest-leverage readability fix.
    71	- **Default sustained reading to ragged text.** Justification needs language-aware
    72	  hyphenation, adequate measure, and rendered review; without those, it creates rivers.
    73	- **Control web-font layout shift.** Pair `font-display` with
    74	  a **metric-matched fallback** (`size-adjust` + `ascent/descent/line-gap-override`)
    75	  and measure the result. Metric matching reduces font-driven reflow; it does not
    76	  justify a zero-CLS claim without observation.
    77	
    78	## Role map and metric budget
    79	
    80	Build a role map before naming faces. It distinguishes scanning work from sustained
    81	reading while keeping both recognizably part of one product: operational labels,
    82	identifiers, and tables need stable width, clear wraps, and numeric behavior; public
    83	narrative needs a calmer measure, stronger editorial hierarchy, and a reading rhythm
    84	that does not leak into dense controls. The role map records each role’s reading job,
    85	size and measure, language coverage, numeric feature, fallback, and verification path.
    86	
    87	Test language coverage with a real longest translation, accented characters, punctuation,
    88	and the dense labels that create wrapping pressure. Test numeric tables with the values
    89	users compare, including tabular figures where alignment carries meaning. A declared
    90	font family is only a hypothesis until the loaded font and fallback both render those
    91	specimens without clipping, substitution, or an unstable hierarchy.
    92	
    93	For every loaded face, keep a metric budget: chosen fallback, size or metric overrides,
    94	critical text that may reflow, and the observed layout-shift result. A system stack is a
    95	valid final choice when it suits the role; a custom face is a valid choice when its
    96	coverage and metric proof justify its cost. In either case, phrase missing measurements
    97	as a verification gate, never as a zero-CLS claim.
    98	
    99	## Quick-start
   100	
   101	Use `assets/starter.css` for the complete scale and metric-matched font example. Keep
   102	the operating constraints here: contextual roles, readable measure, fluid sizing,
   103	language coverage, measured loading/CLS, and no duplicate reduced-motion contract.
   104	### Canonical tokens
   105	
   106	| Token | Role |
   107	|-------|------|
   108	| `--step--2` | Fine print / legal / footnotes |
   109	| `--step--1` | Captions / small labels |
   110	| `--step-0` | Body text |
   111	| `--step-1` through `--step-5` | Headings through display |
   112	| `--font-body` / `--font-display` / `--font-mono` | Font role stacks |
   113	| `--measure` | Max line length (`~66ch`) |
   114	
   115	## Reference files — read the one you need
   116	
   117	Keep this file in context; pull a reference in when the task calls for it.
   118	
   119	- **`references/decision-records.md`** — The distilled *reasoning* of the field: five
   120	  meta-patterns (how typographers think) plus ADR-style decision records linking each
   121	  rule to its rationale and the alternatives it rejects. **Read this when you face a
   122	  novel judgment the other files don't directly cover** — the meta-patterns let you
   123	  derive the right answer the way an expert would. Also the home of the "principle,
   124	  not property" boundary.
   125	- **`references/foundations.md`** — The classical principles (Lupton's *Letter /
   126	  Text / Grid*, Bringhurst, Butterick) translated to the web: anatomy, measure,
   127	  hierarchy, pairing, alignment. **Read this when choosing/pairing fonts or when a
   128	  layout "feels off" but no single rule is obviously broken.**
   129	- **`references/fluid-scale.md`** — Fluid type with `clamp()`, the `clamp()` math,
   130	  modular-scale ratios, Utopia, container-query units (`cqi`) for component-level
   131	  type. **Read this when building or adjusting a type scale, or making type respond
   132	  to a container rather than the viewport.**
   133	- **`references/font-loading.md`** — Web-font performance: `font-display`,
   134	  preloading, self-hosting vs Google Fonts (GDPR), WOFF2, subsetting,
   135	  `unicode-range`, variable fonts, and **reducing font-driven layout shift with metric-matched
   136	  fallbacks** (`size-adjust`, `*-override`, Fontaine, next/font). **Read this
   137	  whenever a project loads a custom web font or has layout-shift / slow-text issues.**
   138	- **`references/accessibility.md`** — WCAG 2.2 for text: contrast ratios, resize to
   139	  200% (1.4.4), text-spacing overrides (1.4.12), zoom, dyslexia/readability,
   140	  reduced motion. **Read this before claiming type work is done, and any time the
   141	  task mentions accessibility, a11y, contrast, or compliance.**
   142	- **`references/modern-css.md`** — Current CSS type features with browser-support
   143	  status: `text-wrap: balance/pretty`, OpenType (`font-variant-*`,
   144	  `font-feature-settings`), `text-box-trim`/`text-box-edge`, `hanging-punctuation`,
   145	  `initial-letter`, font-relative units (`lh`, `cap`, `ch`, `ic`), `line-clamp`,
   146	  `color-mix` for tints. **Read this when reaching for a polish feature, and to
   147	  check whether something needs progressive enhancement.**
   148	
   149	## How to deliver
   150	
   151	Explain the type decision, edit existing tokens, and keep unsupported features progressive. Report contrast, 200% zoom, text-spacing, loading, and glyph evidence; pair multilingual work with `i18n-ready`.
   152

   154	
   155	Close with a five-field evidence ledger. Put the check ID at the start of Reason, then
   156	record either an observed result or an explicit verification gate.
   157	
   158	| Status | Reason | Remediation | Evidence | Provenance |
   159	| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
   160	|  | role-map — contextual type stance and language coverage |  |  |  |

   162	|  | metrics — loading and fallback measurement |  |  |  |

   164	|  | handoff — responsive-layout boundary |  |  |  |

   172	- Exclude: Do not choose a font from examples instead of the brief (+1 in contract.json)

     1	---
     2	name: spacing-system
     3	description: >-
     4	  Use when layout rhythm, density, or gaps feel arbitrary or inconsistent, or when one
     5	  product needs different reading pressures without splitting into unrelated spacing
     6	  systems.
     7	---
     8	
     9	# Spacing System (the scale nobody owns)
    10	
    11	Every gap uses one `--space-*` scale; named semantic relationships create composition
    12	instead of arbitrary margins.
    13	
    14	## The decision order
    15	
    16	1. Publish one 4px-base ladder: `4/8/12/16/24/32/48/64/96px` as `--space-1` through
    17	   `--space-9`, plus `--space-section: clamp(48px, 32px + 4vw, 96px)`.
    18	2. Map attachment, control, task, group, region, and chapter relationships to visibly different steps
    19	   (roughly ≥1.5× between levels).
    20	3. Choose metronomic or intentional syncopated section rhythm from content pressure.
    21	4. Use `gap` and one-direction logical margins; do not stack arbitrary margins.
    22	
    23	## Let content set the rhythm
    24	
    25	Map repeated operational tasks to compact predictability and editorial/narrative content
    26	to deliberate breathing room; retain one system and state why named roles differ. Use the
    27	role map before picking a value:
    28	
    29	| Relationship | Shared token | Compact operational rhythm | Long-form editorial rhythm |
    30	| --- | --- | --- | --- |
    31	| attachment | `--space-1` (4px) | icon/state to its label | inline metadata pair |
    32	| control | `--space-2/3` (8/12px) | label-to-control / inside a control | claim-to-citation / tight evidence pair |
    33	| task | `--space-4` (16px) | repeated queue item | adjacent paragraph or evidence group |
    34	| group | `--space-5` (24px) | task-group break | discrete finding |
    35	| region | `--space-6/7/8` (32/48/64px) | console-region break | narrative subsection or causal pivot |
    36	| chapter | `--space-section` (fluid) | major workflow view | major account transition |
    37	
    38	The console may be metronomic; an account may widen only at finding, subsection, and
    39	chapter boundaries. That is one vocabulary with different relationships, not two scales.
    40	
    41	## Non-negotiables
    42	
    43	- Audit `margin`/`padding`/`gap` for tokens (except legitimate `0`/`auto`/relative prose).
    44	- Proximity must tell the truth; `--space-section` is fluid and each context has one base.
    45	- Whitespace is an intentional structural move, never inconsistent leftover space.
    46	
    47	## Migration and honest audit boundary
    48	
    49	Classify every literal by its relationship before replacement: `13px` becomes `12px`
    50	(`--space-3`), `17px` becomes `16px` (`--space-4`), and a bare `24px` layout value becomes
    51	`var(--space-5)`. Treat `19px` as failed until its observed selector establishes task (16px)
    52	or group (24px); do not silently round it. The sole prose exception is documented following-
    53	paragraph `1em` spacing, because it is relative to type rather than a layout primitive.
    54	
    55	Use `assets/spacing-starter.css` as a fixture and starting point. Without a CSS fixture in
    56	the target project, mark the audit `PENDING`, name it and the next audit action; never claim
    57	that residual off-scale values are gone. The ladder, map, and migration rules remain required.
    58	
    59	## Completion evidence
    60	
    61	Audit the source before describing it and map each value to a role. Handoff one completed
    62	Markdown table with `status`, `reason`, `remediation`, `evidence`, and `provenance`, covering
    63	`scale`, `off-scale`, `proximity`, `rhythm`, and `handoff`. `PENDING` names its missing source
    64	and next action; it blocks that check rather than acting as placeholder proof.
    65	
    66	## Semantic rhythm map
    67	
    68	Name attachment, control, task, group, region, and chapter relationships, then show how
    69	compact/editorial contexts select them without parallel scales. Each arbitrary value is
    70	replaced, justified as an observed exception, or left PENDING with a source and next action.
    71	
    72	## Authoritative self-check
    73	
    74	Include exactly one labeled **Authoritative self-check** in the delivery. It confirms the
    75	complete ladder and exact section clamp, all six relationships, operational versus editorial
    76	role selection, the `13/17/19/bare-24` migration decision, and the honest fixture boundary.
    77	This check is evidence-based, not a length target or a duplicate summary.
    78	
    79	## How to deliver
    80	
    81	Deliver base, token scale, role map, context rhythm, off-scale audit, and the one authoritative
    82	self-check. Reject `responsive-layout` as the primary route because it owns breakpoint reflow,
    83	not the scale, proximity, or reading-pressure decisions; hand it `--space-*` and the completed
    84	evidence table. Send control and form relationships to `component-states` or `form-ux`, rather
    85	than re-deciding their behavior.
    86	
    87	## Reference files
    88	
    89	- `references/decision-records.md` — novel-case ADR rules.
    90	- `assets/spacing-starter.css` — copy-paste ladder, layout primitives, and migration helpers.
    91

    98	- Exclude: Do not impose one uniform cadence across dissimilar content (+1 in contract.json)
